# Source register: 1808/9 unidentified eruption search

Living list of every source examined or identified (literature, periodicals, travelogues, diaries, logs, archive series, catalogues). Updated as the search proceeds; per-strand detail lives in `notes/`.

**Mac folder moved (27 Oct 2026):** everything formerly in ~/Downloads/1808-9 Volcano Project/, ~/Downloads/1808-newspapers and ~/Downloads/1808-claude-work is now in ~/Documents/1808-9 Volcano Project/ (papers, books, newspapers, archives, logbooks, data, scripts). Older paths in notes resolve through mac_folder_path_map_2026-10-27.csv; layout in mac_project_folder_README.md.

**Access column** — *Accessed*: text actually read from here (say how: page image / OCR text / modern transcription). *Digitized, not reached*: scans exist but were blocked or not navigable from this environment. *Catalogue-only*: a catalogue record, no scan found. *Not digitized / unknown*.

Environment note: this research environment can reach the web only through a text-fetching tool (no direct downloads), so many image viewers (Hemeroteca Digital Brasileira docreader, HathiTrust page viewer, archive IIIF viewers) may be readable only partially or not at all. "Not reached" here is often a tool limit, not proof the item is offline.

**Unavailable sources** (offline, paywalled, blocked, or needing William's hands): see `unavailable_sources.md` (numbered U1...; standing rule 27 Oct 2026: every thread adds such sources there in the same turn).

Newspaper access paths (URLs, accounts, download and OCR options per title, 16 Oct 2026): see `newspaper_access_guide.md`. The download script (`scripts/fetch_newspapers.py`, run on William's Mac) was confirmed on 16 Oct to download the *Diario de México* and *Gazeta de México* page PDFs from HNDM (331 and 132 issues in Sept 1808–Jul 1809) and the *Gazeta de Madrid* from BOE. The images are readable: the *Diario* of 5 Sept 1808, p. 1, was checked. Section 5 of that file compares paid aggregators; the two best bets for volume in the window are the British Newspaper Archive (London and port papers, 1808–09) and Readex America's Historical Newspapers (US port ship reports, library-only). It found that the *Gazeta Ministerial de Sevilla* (Jun 1808–Jan 1809) and the *Gazeta del Gobierno* (1809) are on BOE, and that the Bogotá *Redactor Americano* (1806–09) is also a window title.

**Read 16 Oct 2026 (page images, William's downloads):** York Factory B.239/a/114 (1807–08), 115 (London copy 1808–09), 116 (1809–10), 117 (1810–11), 119 (post copy 1808–c.1812). One anomaly: 4 Nov 1808 "The darkest day I have seen in Hudsons Bay" (119, PDF p8L), cause not stated; no haze, sun-colour or aurora remarks anywhere (low sensitivity). Details: notes/hbc_york_factory_journals.md. Next: Churchill, Severn and Moose journals for 3–5 Nov 1808.

**Read 16 Oct 2026 (page images):** Churchill B.42/a/133–136A (key vol. 134, 23 Aug 1808 – 2 Sept 1809): no dark day 4 Nov, entries thin; −40 to −45 °F 13–15 Jan 1809. Moose B.135/a/95–99 (key vol. 97, 19 Sept 1808 – 26 Sept 1809, London copy; rough journal 96 stops 6 Oct 1808): "remarkably dark" 1 Nov with heavy rain, dark and rainy 5 Nov, warm thaw early Nov. Supports a weather explanation for York's 4 Nov dark day. Notes: notes/hbc_churchill_journals.md, notes/hbc_moose_journals.md. Severn not yet read.

**Edmonton House B.60/a/7–9 read 16 Oct 2026 (page images):** James Bird's irregular "Sketch" summaries, 19 Jul 1807 – 2 Aug 1809 and 28 Sep 1810 – 30 Jul 1811; no 1809–10 volume. No entries 2–5 Nov 1808; no sky remarks or thermometer readings at all. Deep snow winter 1808–09; river froze 14 Nov 1808, broke 15 Apr 1809. Notes: notes/hbc_edmonton_journals.md.

**"Not reached" entries are an access limit of this environment, not evidence that the source is undigitized or silent.**

### E2. East India Company access check (16 Oct 2026)
[Update 16 Oct, B39 cross-check: Royal George journal (IOR/L/MAR/B/17L) also read from QDL page images, Whampoa/Canton 22 Jul 1808 – 5 Apr 1809, weak negative: notes/royal_george_canton_jul1808-apr1809.md. Syracuse University Bird Library offers walk-in visitor access to Readex America's Historical Newspapers and Caribbean Newspapers (email libref@syr.edu); details in newspaper_access_guide.md.]
See [notes/eic_access.md](notes/eic_access.md). Wexford journal read 2 Aug 1808–25 Mar 1809 from page images (negative): [notes/wexford_journal_aug1808-mar1809.md](notes/wexford_journal_aug1808-mar1809.md). Reachable here: BL catalogue text; CDMP keyed EIC logbook weather (29 ships, Oct 1808–Mar 1809, in data/eic_keyed_weather_1808-10_1809-03.csv, negative for haze/ash anomalies but remarks not keyed); Asiatic Annual Register 1808–10 OCR (negative). Not reachable: QDL page images for Wexford (free in a browser), BL manuscripts (on-site in London), NCEI log images (login).
- Extension to Mar–Sept 1808 (16 Oct): keyed EIC weather for Mar–Sept 1808 (703 records, 24 ships; data/eic_keyed_weather_1808-03_1808-09.csv) shows no haze excess. Unkeyed harbour stays mean the Canton/Whampoa (Sept 1808–Mar 1809), Bengal, Bombay and Madras logs for the key window are unread (data/eic_unkeyed_port_stays_1808.txt). **Royal George journal, IOR/L/MAR/B/17L**, at Whampoa 5 Jul 1808–Mar 1809: on QDL https://www.qdl.qa/en/archive/81055/vdc_100000001320.0x000340, PDF https://www.qdl.qa/download/pdf/81055_vdc_100000001320.0x000340_en.pdf (410 MB, browser only). **Downloaded by William 16 Oct; Whampoa/Canton 22 Jul 1808 to Penang 5 Apr 1809 read from page images: negative but weak (one formulaic weather clause a day, sky colour never noted).** [notes/royal_george_canton_jul1808-apr1809.md](notes/royal_george_canton_jul1808-apr1809.md). The other China-fleet logs (Alnwick Castle 189D, Elphinstone 201C, Essex 229T, Canton 288H, Warley 182H) are BL only. Wexford 19 Feb–1 Aug 1808 (QDL 0135–0174) read from page images, negative (seasonal haze only): [notes/wexford_journal_feb-jul1808.md](notes/wexford_journal_feb-jul1808.md). The journal is now covered continuously 19 Feb 1808–25 Mar 1809.
- Mar–Aug 1808 log sweep (16 Oct): 85 further RN (TNA ADM 51/52, other repositories) and EIC (BL) log pieces covering ships east of India, none digitised or read: [catalogue_sweep_mar-aug1808.md](catalogue_sweep_mar-aug1808.md). Top: Surat Castle 205F (Straits–Canton), Windham 230G and Walmer Castle 181F (Bencoolen), Terpsichore, Doris ex-Pitt/Salsette, Cornwallis.
- **HMS Greyhound (32), wrecked on the Zambales coast of Luzon, 4 or 11 Oct 1808** (lead from Gazeta de México 25 Oct 1809 pp. 964–965; checked 16 Oct, TNA Discovery API). No captain's or master's log for 1808 is catalogued: the captain's logs stop 8 Jan 1808 (ADM 51/1883, C4458968; earlier ADM 51/1760), master's logs stop 1807 (ADM 52/3832, 52/4033); the 1808 log was probably lost with the ship. Capt. William Pakenham took command off Pulo Penang 17 Aug 1808 (ADM 1/2333/11, C12840170). Court martial requested Mar–Apr 1810 at Portsmouth (ADM 1/2336/70, /79; ADM 1/1807/31); Pakenham says on 11 Jul 1810 the proceedings "are now at the Admiralty", loss date 11 Oct 1808 (ADM 1/2336/137, C12840726). The proceedings themselves are not itemised in Discovery; expected in the ADM 1 courts-martial series for mid-1810 (piece number unconfirmed). Also: imprest for Manila provisions after the loss (ADM 1/2336/66). Printed accounts: Grocott, *Shipwrecks of the Revolutionary and Napoleonic Eras* pp. 261–262 (date given there as 4 Oct 1808, via mq.edu.au ships list) and *The Times* 15 Jul 1809. None digitised; all TNA Kew on site. October is typhoon season off Luzon, so a cause in the record would need care.
- **Spanish frigate Susana, Manila → Île de France with Manila's dispatches of 17 Oct 1808** (lead from Decaen's letter, Gazeta de México Dec 1809). Best record: Decaen papers, Bibliothèque municipale (Alexis de Tocqueville) de Caen, Ms. 562 item XCIV "Bâtiments arrivés à l'île de France, du 1er avril 1806 au 25 avril 1809" (194 pp., French and Portuguese), https://ccfr.bnf.fr/portailccfr/ark:/16871/004D20021239; also item "Correspondances, notes et mémoires sur des colonies" https://ccfr.bnf.fr/portailccfr/ark:/16871/004D20021240. Not digitised as far as the catalogue shows; prior authorisation needed. Gazette de l'Isle de France 1808–09: no online copy found (CRL holds the Mauritius gazette only from 1897; TNA HCA 30 prize papers hold 1792 issues, so captured later issues are possible but none catalogued for 1808–09). TNA Discovery has nothing under "Susana Manila" 1807–11. Spanish side (Archivo General de Indias / Museo Naval, Real Compañía de Filipinas) not yet searched.

### B44. Royal Society Krakatoa report (1888), historical list of coloured twilights, 1805-1812 (26 Oct 2026; notes/krakatoa_report_historical_twilights_2026-10-26.md)

Result: **negative.** No coloured twilight, dry fog, blue or dim sun anywhere between 6 May 1808 and 27 Mar 1809. All 1808-1812 entries are Luke Howard's London notes; the 1809 one (27 Mar, 1-2 Apr) was already known (notes/nonIberian_fulltext_pass2.md §1A). Howard's day counts: 1810 = 4, 1811 = 33; the 1811 skies start 16 Jun, 3 days after the Sabrina (Azores) eruption, which Howard prints alongside. Assessment (inference): the 1811 twilights are not a late phase of the 1809 veil. Verification: OCR only, two archive.org OCRs agree; printed page = leaf - 116.

### B45. Long Island and Bismarck Sea neighbours, c. 1660-1953 (26 Oct 2026; notes/long_island_post_tibito_2026-10-26.md)

Result: no eruption of Long Island or its neighbours is recorded 1700-1827. No PNG volcano sighting of any kind is dated 1797-1826 (Johnson 2013). A large Long Island eruption in 1808 is excluded: there is no second highland ash, and Long Island's own VEI 6 gave only about 4 Tg S (Gabriel 2025). A small eruption c. 1780-1840 is hinted at by D'Urville's arid shore (1827) and by resettlers who found the island treeless and steaming (1840s-50s). It is undated and could not be the 1809 source. Verification as marked.

### B46. Antarctic per-core 1809 vs Tambora sulfate map (26 Oct 2026; notes/antarctic_sulfate_map_2026-10-26.md, data/antarctic_sulfate_1809_vs_tambora.csv)

Result: away from Victoria Land the 1809/Tambora flux ratio is uniform (15 cores, mean 0.48, sd 0.12; Claude's calculation). **Talos Dome (TD96 1.05, TALDICE 1.11) is about 5 sd high**, about 245 km from Mt Melbourne and the Pleiades; GV7 (450 km) is normal at 0.57. This fits a small local Victoria Land eruption adding sulfate c. 1809-10 (cf. Kurbatov 2006 Siple Dome glass), not the main source (inference). Talos values page-image checked 26 Oct (Nardin 2020 p. 8; William's PDF in `nardin_2020_gv7/`): core assignment confirmed; the two Talos cores agree on 1809 although they disagree on Reclus and Samalas. Greenland ratios 0.55-0.96.

### B47. Lunar eclipses 3 Nov 1808 and 30 Apr 1809: colour/darkness search (26 Oct 2026; notes/lunar_eclipses_1808-09_2026-10-26.md)
Circumstances (NASA LE1801-1900): 3 Nov 1808 total (mag 1.51, greatest 08:14 UT), invisible in Europe, seen from the Americas 01:30-04:00 local, Pacific, at moonrise in Japan/Sydney; 30 Apr 1809 **partial** (mag 0.87, 00:33 UT), well placed for Europe, Africa, S. America; 23 Oct 1809 partial (0.83). Overall: **no colour or darkness report found for any eclipse May 1808-1810** (weak negative; main astronomical journals not searchable here).

### B50. Glass and rock compositions of blank-zone candidates, scored against the 1809 ice glass (26 Oct 2026; notes/glass_candidates_compilation_2026-10-26.md, data/glass_candidates.csv, data/glass_similarity_2026-10-26.csv)
Result (Claude's calculation; conclusions inference): no candidate glass matches. The closest family to the Dome C and South Pole 1809 glass is the Halmahera arc. Dukono whole rock scores 0.89-0.93, above Tibito glass at 0.88, but whole rock is not glass, and Dukono's glass would be more silicic than the ice glass. K2O (ice 2.0-3.1) argues against the low-K arcs: Tonga, the New Britain arc front and Witori. No major-element data were found for the tier-B Banda volcanoes, and no glass for any tier-B New Britain volcano. GEOROC and EarthChem downloads were unreachable. Plunkett 2023's "Telaga Group F" comparison rests on bulk tephra of a c. 14 ka Ternate maar (Faral 2022 Group F), not on glass.
**Update 26 Oct ~22:15 UTC (rows below supersede the result line above):** the GEOROC and Gill files were later downloaded by William and scored (8,740 + 248 analyses). Southern Banda (Teon, Nila, Wetar) is medium-K and not excluded; only the northern Banda Api-Manuk-Serua group is low-K. Many unrelated medium-K arc glasses score 0.90-0.95, so the desk glass comparison has reached its limit (notes sec. 9).

### B52. Candidate profiles: Dakataua, southern Banda arc, Cabalían, Gaua, Awu, Ibu, Dukono (26 Oct 2026; notes/candidate_profiles_dakataua_sbanda_2026-10-26.md)
All text fetch or file; no page images. Result (inference): no published deposit at these volcanoes is dated to c. 1808. Dakataua: only Dk (c. 1350-1270 cal BP) and a possible c. 800 CE unit are dated. The "at least five" post-caldera eruptions are undated. The latest (Makalia) rests on 1963 testimony, "about 1880-1890". Teon and Nila: no record 1693-1903/04 and no dated young deposits. Snellius-II charcoal from Teon's "1660" ash flows has no published date found. Cabalían (Leyte): 14C 135 ± 30 BP on a pyroclastic flow (GVP "1820 ± 30") lies on the calibration plateau, roughly 1680-1940 cal AD, so it includes 1808. It has no composition. Corrections to B50 sec. 9: "Awu 0.92" is a name-match on Mahawu (Awu ≤0.82); "Ibu 0.91" is the Lake Todoke sample (Ibu ≤0.78); the GEOROC "Wetar" rows are Wetar island (Neogene), so Gunungapi Wetar is unsampled.

### B53. Candidate volcano priority list (26 Oct 2026; notes/candidate_priority_list_2026-10-26.md)
Synthesis of B45, B46, B48-B52; no new sources. Ranking is Claude's judgment (inference); scores are Claude's calculation; tests are suggestions. Criteria per candidate: glass score (K2O as the only discriminating filter), size capability, dated deposit near 1808 (none known anywhere; only Cabalían's 14C date covers 1808), local-record coverage, ice constraints (southern source weakly favoured; Antarctic ratio uniform, Talos local). **Top tier:** (1) Dakataua/Makalia (only blank-zone glass at 0.92; caldera; no surviving written account known; test: Lake Dakataua sediment core, desk step Machida 1996); (2) Gaua (WR 0.94/0.91; undated caldera, 14°S; test: date youngest caldera tephra); (3) Cabalían (no composition, probably small, but a 14C date spanning 1808; test: Cabalían/Sogod parish and mission records 1808-09). **Possible:** Agung (Raffles's "about seven years ago" report, not scored), Todoko-Ranu, Dukono-Tarakani, Teon, Nila, N Sulawesi (Mahawu, Lokon-Empung), Tondano, Tavui, Negros, Balbi, Lamington, Victory; plus untested blank-zone centres. **Ruled out or unlikely:** Tonga, New Britain arc front, Witori, Rabaul glass, N Banda, Long Island, Ibu, Awu, Soputan/Klabat/Ambang, Matthew/Hunter, shield basalts, Tambo Quemado, watched-zone tier A, Victoria Land (local only). findings.csv not changed (it records observations; F20 already holds the glass result).

### B54. American whaling and merchant logbooks, Sep 1808 to mid-1810 (27 Oct 2026; notes/american_logbooks_2026-10-27.md)
Complete. Four digitized logs read in full as page images (William downloaded the PDFs): Cyrus (London whaler, ex-Rotch, Paul West; S Atlantic, Cape Horn, Chile, Peru grounds, Galápagos, St Helena; 9 Sep 1808-23 Jul 1810), Hero (Westport whaler, Latham Paddock; S Atlantic, SE Africa, Delagoa Bay, Saldanha Bay; 3 Nov 1808-22 Feb 1810), Sukey (Nantucket whaler, G. W. Gardner; homeward N Atlantic Sep-Nov 1808, then Cape Horn, Peru, Galápagos 9 Jul 1809-Aug 1810), Fame of Salem (Benjamin Bullock; Lisbon, Cádiz, Algeciras Oct 1809-Feb 1810, then Indian Ocean via Île St Paul to the W Sumatra pepper coast, Nias, Singkil, to 16 Jul 1810). **No ash, pumice, detonations, darkness, dimmed or red sun, odd twilights or sea waves in any of them (page image).** No log was in the Indonesia/Melanesia/central Pacific zone during Dec 1808-1809; Fame reached Sumatra only Jun-Jul 1810. Weather items worth keeping, none volcanic (Claude's readings): Cyrus 20 Mar-4 Apr 1809 two weeks of heavy rain and dark weather at 2.5-5.5S, 84-89W ("very much like the bay of Panama"), a warm-water pattern; Cyrus 18-23 Aug 1809 Galápagos parched by "extreme heat and drought... for two seasons past"; Cyrus 11-15 Mar 1810 St Helena rollers wrecked the piers (swell, not tsunami); Sukey 18 Apr 1810 "saw a fire on the Mountain" of Charles I. (Floreana, no historical volcano; brush or people's fire); Fame 11-17 Mar 1810 discoloured water and "smoaky hazy weather as usual" off Cape Verde (Saharan dust). AOWV voyage database (whalinghistory.org, voyages_20260619): c. 204 American voyages overlap 1808-10, almost all on Brazil, Patagonia, Chile/Peru, Galápagos and Cape/Saldanha grounds, none in the Indonesian/Melanesian zone; AOWL position data has one voyage in the window (BV017710, no remarks); no other scanned logs for 1808-10. Free US newspapers searched again with maritime terms: negative (OCR).

### B55. Bipolar sulfate lag and hemispheric ratio, 1809 vs Tambora and Krakatau (27 Oct 2026; notes/bipolar_sulfate_lag_2026-10-27.md, data/bipolar_lag_1809_2026-10-27.csv, scripts/bipolar_lag_2026-10-27/lag.py)
Complete (desk calculation from published tables; no raw download possible today; text read, not page images). Greenland minus Antarctic onset lag for 1809 is −0.05 yr (range −0.4 to +0.3; ±0.35 per pair) across WDC06A, DSS, NEEM-S1 and NGRIP. Tambora gives +0.25, Krakatau −0.7. The deposition is synchronous, so the source is tropical; the lag cannot place it north or south. The NH/SH split relative to Tambora is about 1.4 (0.9-2.2), so roughly 55% of the deposited sulfate fell in the north. That fits a Dec-Jan eruption or a near-equatorial/northern source; it does not exclude the southern candidates. The Tambora-analogue eruption date is 1809.07 ± 0.41. Burke 2019 did not measure 1809.

### B58. Coral and tree-ring fingerprints: Timmreck 2021 in full, later citing work, ENSO state 1808-1810 (27 Oct 2026; notes/coral_treering_enso_1809_2026-10-27.md, data/coral_enso_1804-1814_2026-10-27.csv, scripts/coral_enso_2026-10-27/coral_enso.py)

Verification: [text] = fetched via web text tool, no page images; [calc] = Claude's calculation from published values. Result: Timmreck 2021 put every run at the Equator on 1 Jan 1809 and tested no other latitude or season; its NH-only-removal run (nNHP) fits the tree rings best but is judged unlikely. Later work (Marshall 2025, Sanchez 2021, Wilson 2026 preprint) names no hemisphere; Wilson 2026 dates the event 1808. ENSO: tree rings and multiproxy lists say La Niña 1808-1810, corals say El Niño-like Dec 1808 to May 1809, all neutral/La Niña-like by 1810; Lombok coral normal Jul-Nov 1809. Claude's inference: El Niño unlikely to explain the 1809 Batavia dry-season drought.

### B62. Creative next steps: calibrated deposition-ratio latitude band, GVP detection base rate, Indo-Pacific coral footprint, Vietnamese court chronicle (28 Oct 2026; notes/creative_next_steps_2026-10-28.md, data/deposition_emulator_ratio_by_latitude_2026-10-28.csv, data/coral_footprint_1806-1816_2026-10-28.csv, scripts/creative_next_steps_2026-10-28/)
Strategy note with 25 numbered options and 13 side problems. New results, all Claude's calculations from downloaded published data [calc] unless stated: (1) the Marshall et al. 2021 Greenland/Antarctic deposition emulator, inverted for the 1809 composites, gives 17-30°S raw, but the emulator and the full UM-UKCA Tambora run (Marshall 2018 Table 5 [text]) put about twice too much of a tropical eruption's sulfate on Greenland; calibrated by the observed 1809/Tambora ratio-of-ratios (1.2, range 0.9-2.2) the source band is roughly 14-3°S for a January eruption, 4°S-16°N for July, so about 12°S-3°N for a Nov-Dec eruption, which keeps Dakataua, the Banda arc, Agung, Halmahera and N Sulawesi, leaves Gaua at the edge and excludes Cabalían unless the eruption was mid-year; validation with Huaynaputina, Parker, Cosigüina, Krakatau needs the eVolv2k supplement (U36). (2) GVP VOTW 4.6.7: 30 VEI>=5 eruptions 1500-1900, 26 historically observed; two of the six VEI>=6 (Billy Mitchell c. 1580, Long Island c. 1660) have no written record, both Melanesian. (3) PAGES2k v2 corals: Lombok Strait (13 km from Agung) Dec 1808 +2.5 sd, 1809/10 to 1812/13 +1.0 to +1.75 sd yearly, seven of the twelve most extreme months of 1782-1990 in 1810-12; Fiji, Abraham Reef, New Caledonia, Galápagos, Malindi all cool/dry 1809-12; Palau and Guam weak. (4) Đại Nam thực lục 2002 translation scan located (image only; scans c. 735-790 = Nov 1808 to mid-1810; two pages read as page images to map the years). EVA_H Python code cloned. Ten sources added to unavailable_sources.md (U34-U39, U65, U66, U85, U98).

### B63. Cabalían parish records on FamilySearch: coverage check and Safari route to the nearest books covering 1808-10 (28 Oct 2026; notes/cabalian_parish_records_familysearch_2026-10-28.md)
Option 10 of B62. All results are catalogue or wiki text fetched 28 Oct 2026 [text]; no register image was opened. Result: the filmed registers of San Juan (the former Cabalian) begin in 1861 (FamilySearch catalogue 11677, "Microfilm of manuscripts in San Juan, Southern Leyte"), Sogod in 1869 (11636; burials 1869-1896, film 1357187, DGS 7771951), Macrohon 1862 (10668), Maasin 1871 (85895), Baybay 1813 (434156), Hindang and Matalom 1843, Hilongos 1864; Liloan and Hinunangan have no entry found; the Archdiocese of Palo parishes (Abuyog, Dulag, Palo and the rest of eastern and northern Leyte) are not on FamilySearch at all. No Leyte register covers 1808-10, so the Cabalían burial-spike test cannot be done from digitised images and the pre-1861 books were probably lost before filming (inference). The nearest filmed books that do cover the window: Guiuan, Eastern Samar (100 km NE; 1782-1956; 657877), seven Bohol parishes 150-180 km WSW (Panglao 1697, Tagbilaran 1742, Baclayon 1747, Dauis 1752, Loon 1753, Maribojoc 1767, Cortes 1769), southern and central Cebu 140-205 km W (Lapu-Lapu 1724, Carcar 1759, Mabolo 1770, Boljoon 1793, Dalaguete 1800, Barili 1809) and Tanjay, Negros (241 km; 1800); Llorente 1811 and Baybay 1813 start just after. These give a regional test of a large Cabalían eruption on both the downwind (NE-monsoon, Bohol) and the opposite (Guiuan) sides (reasoning in the note, section 4; suggestion). Distances are Claude's calculation from the GVP coordinates. The step-by-step Safari route is in the note, section 5. unavailable_sources.md: U35 superseded, U84 amended, U86, U87, U120-U122 added. findings.csv and the priority list unchanged (no observation of 1808-10 recorded).

### B64. Đại Nam thực lục, Gia Long 7-9 (1808-10), read as page images (28 Oct 2026; notes/dai_nam_thuc_luc_1808-10_2026-10-28.md, scripts/dai_nam_thuc_luc_2026-10-28/luncal.py)

Thread "Read the Vietnamese court chronicle 1808-10" (option 2 of B62). The 2002 Viện Sử học translation (tập 1, image-only scan on vietsu.org, U39) was downloaded into Claude's cloud container and scans 719-810 (printed pp. 716-807, quyển XXXIV-XLII, 28 Jan 1808 to Feb 1811) were read as page images; 55 weather, disaster, harvest, price and relief entries are tabulated with lunar dates converted to Gregorian [calc, sxtwl, cross-checked on 30 sexagenary day names and the solar eclipse of 4 Apr 1810 which the chronicle records]. Results [page image]: no sun, moon, sky-colour, darkness, ashfall, boom or earthquake entry in three years (the eclipse is the only sky entry); 1809 autumn crop in the Red River delta lost to "nắng hạn lâu ngày" (long drought) in some places and "gió bão nước mặn" (typhoon, salt water) in others (edict Jan 1810, p. 771), typhoon with sea-water inundation on the Hải Dương-Yên Quảng-Sơn Nam hạ coast Oct-Nov 1809 (p. 766), famine in Bắc Thành from Oct-Nov 1809 to Jul 1810 "liền năm đói kém" with rice dear and coin short (pp. 772, 783, 786, 789); Nghệ An famine Jan-Feb 1809 after a 1808 drought year, Quảng Bình famine Feb-Mar 1809; drought Quảng Nam-Quảng Ngãi Mar-Apr 1810, Bắc Thành Jul 1810, Nghệ An Jan 1811; typhoon at Huế Aug 1810. Pre-eruption drought in Quảng Đức, Bình Định, Phú Yên May-Jun 1808 shows the chronicle reports drought routinely. Calendar: the chronicle has a leap 6th month in 1808 (Qing: leap 5th) and says in May 1810 that Vietnam's Vạn Toàn calendar still used the Ming Datong method [page image]; month starts coincide with the Qing calendar for every dated entry [calc]. Administrative orders of 1808-09 set up monthly provincial reports of rice prices and "nắng mưa" (pointer to the Gia Long châu bản, U88). The 1809 drought at 21°N joins the Guangdong-Guangxi (B60) and Java (B51) droughts of 1809. Suggested control: read Gia Long 14-15 (Tambora) in the same PDF. Added U67, U88, U123; U39 amended.

### B65. Coral footprint of 1809-1812: the three option-4 papers, D'Arrigo 2006 and Tierney 2015 as prior art, Vanuatu added, map (28 Oct 2026; notes/coral_footprint_1809-12_2026-10-28.md, data/coral_footprint_1800-1820_2026-10-28.csv, data/coral_footprint_external_series_2026-10-28.csv, data/coral_footprint_monthly_z_1800-1820_2026-10-28.json, data/coral_footprint_map_1809-12_2026-10-28.png, data/coral_footprint_series_1805-1818_2026-10-28.png, scripts/coral_footprint_2026-10-28/)

Thread "Write up the 1809-12 coral footprint" (option 4 of B62). Charles et al. 2003, Linsley et al. 2006 and Bagnato et al. 2005 read in full through the fetch tool [text]: none discusses 1808-1812; Linsley 2006 treats Tambora only (SPCZ "shifted north and/or contracted for almost a decade" after 1815, Rarotonga δ18O +0.4 per mil 1814-23 "not observed in Fiji"). Prior art found: D'Arrigo et al. 2006 warm-pool SON SST (Lombok coral + Java teak before 1863) gives 1809-12 = −0.28, −0.73, −0.76, −0.79 °C against −0.30, −0.51, −0.51 for 1815-17 and calls the unknown eruption the larger of the two influences; Tierney et al. 2015 find the western Pacific's coolest decade 1808-17 (−0.5 °C) and the Indian Ocean's 1807-16 (−0.8 °C), attributed to the two eruptions and the Dalton minimum. Claude's recomputation of all 34 PAGES2k coral records with data in 1806-16 [calc]: Lombok July 1809-June 1813 +1.33 sd, the most extreme four-year window of 1782-1990, nine of the twelve most extreme months in 1810-12, excursion starting December 1808 (+2.1 sd) with a normal 1809 dry season; Palau also jumps in December 1808 (+2.2); Guam cool from June 1808; Fiji (four records) +0.73, Abraham Reef +1.24, Malindi +0.88, Galápagos +1.28; Vanuatu (Quinn 1996, 98 km from Gaua, annual, 2-4 samples a year) shows no cool/dry excursion (−0.6 to −1.3 sd, warm/wet); Tonga, Rarotonga, Madagascar, W Australia, Caribbean, Red Sea nothing or opposite; Queensland rainfall (Lough 2007) 99-117 % of mean in 1809-12, no drought. Eastern Pacific cool in 1810-11 (La Niña-like) while the Maritime Continent stayed dry: not an ENSO pattern [inference]. Corals do not locate the eruption; they document a cool-dry excursion that outlasted Tambora's at Lombok (M13). Two sources added to unavailable_sources.md (U127 Quinn 1996 full text, U69 Quinn 1993).

### B66. Hemispheric sulfate-ratio latitude band tested and withdrawn: Marshall 2021 emulator validated against eleven known eruptions, EVA_H runs (28 Oct 2026; notes/deposition_ratio_validation_2026-10-28.md, data/deposition_ratio_validation_2026-10-28.csv, data/deposition_ratio_of_ratios_vs_tambora_2026-10-28.csv, data/evah_deposition_ratio_grid_2026-10-28.csv, data/evah_saod_timing_2026-10-28.csv, data/evolv2k_v2.1_events_2026-10-28.csv, scripts/deposition_validation_2026-10-28/)
Option 1 of B62 executed. All numbers Claude's calculations from downloaded published data [calc]; sources read as fetched text, not page images. (1) The provisional band of B62 (12°S-3°N for a Nov-Dec eruption) is withdrawn: against eleven tropical eruptions of known latitude and month (Huaynaputina, Ruiz, Parker, Gamkonora, Tambora, Cosigüina, Makian, Krakatau, Samalas, Agung, Pinatubo) the Marshall et al. 2021 emulator predicts the Greenland/Antarctic ratio with an rms error of ×3.0 (×2.1 after removing a ×2.3 Greenland bias) and no correlation with the observed ratios (Spearman 0.20, p 0.55); of B62's four named tests only Parker passes within ×1.5 (Krakatau 2.1, Cosigüina 3.2, Huaynaputina 3.4). Marshall 2021's own blind inversion of Tambora lands at 22-56°S for January [text]. (2) The observed ratios have no latitude slope inside ±17° (×1.13 per 10°, p 0.5) but a seasonal one: Oct-Feb eruptions about ×2 more Greenland-heavy than Mar-Jun (p 0.04, n 11); the 1809 ratio (1.3 eVolv2k; 0.9-1.3 across bases) is the lowest of the five Oct-Feb cases (others 2.0-2.8), not significant. (3) EVA_H (default or seasonal mixing) gives NH/SH = 1.00 for any source within ±15° in any month or height; sensitivity begins at ±20°. Its SAOD outputs are latitude-blind and have no transport delay, so useless for the arrival-date question. (4) Consequence: the deposition ratio says only "tropical"; the B62 argument against Cabalían (10°N) is withdrawn; B53 unchanged. (5) Data route found: the per-event ratio is the eVolv2k netCDF variable `hemi`; v2.1 file in GitHub matthew2e/easy-volcanic-aerosol (the supplement, U36, has no flux table, so U36 superseded); v3 needs a WDCC login (U125); Sigl 2015 per-event fluxes with uncertainties (U126).

### B67. Tibet and the Himalaya, 1808-10: written records and plateau natural archives (28 Oct 2026; notes/tibet_himalaya_records_2026-10-28.md, scripts/tibet_icecores_2026-10-28/himalaya_1809.py)

Answer to William's question of 28 Oct whether there are pertinent records from Tibet. Written records [text]: the Kashag natural-disaster compilations begin in 1824 (Palden Gyal 2024); 1808-10 would be in the 9th Dalai Lama's and the regent's biographies (BDRC, Tibetan reader needed, U129) and in Qing amban memorials / Qing Shilu Jiaqing 13-15 (U69); no European was in Tibet in 1808-10 (Manning Dec 1811, Moorcroft Aug 1812); REACHES has no Tibet rows, Sichuan rows give winter 1809/10 snow (B60). Nepal: Documenta Nepalica catalogues Regmi Research Collection documents with English abstracts, incl. a VS 1873 (1816) famine-relief order (Tambora); VS 1865-67 not yet searched (U128). Natural archives [text, data]: Everest East Rongbuk Bi record 1800-2002 (Xu et al. 2009) flags Tambora, not 1809 (nor Krakatau); the sub-annual Everest S and Bi (Kaspari 2009) and Dasuopu trace-element (Gabrielli 2020) NOAA files have never been examined for 1809 and cannot be read here beyond their top 42,000 characters (U127, script ready); Dasuopu decadal Bi enrichment 1790s-1830s flat (4.5, 4.3, 3.4, 4.5, 4.6); Puruogangri 5-yr sulfate dust-dominated; Tibetan tree rings (Gao 2023, Wang 2021, Li 2017, Huang 2025) show the 1810s cold decade and a resilience drop from 1809 but do not isolate 1809 from 1815.

### B68. Everest and Dasuopu ice-core trace-element files, read through William's run of himalaya_1809.py v2.1 (28 Oct 2026, 04:57 UTC; notes/tibet_himalaya_records_2026-10-28.md section 8; scripts/tibet_icecores_2026-10-28/)

[text: pasted terminal output; readings are Claude's] The two NOAA files of U127 (Kaspari et al. 2009 East Rongbuk, 2,280 sub-annual samples 1650-2002; Gabrielli et al. 2020 Dasuopu, 2,564 samples 1499-1992) were scored year by year against 1700-1800 (robust log10 z of yearly medians and maxima, raw and as ratios to Al, Fe, Ca). Everest: 1815 is the only year of 1800-1830 with a raw bismuth spike (max z +4.4; Bi/Al and Bi/Fe +8.2), and 1815-16 is the largest Bi/Fe excursion of 1700-1850, so the core records Tambora; 1809 and 1810 show nothing (raw Bi max -1.0 and -0.2, Bi/Al -0.8 and +0.6, neither year in any top-ten list); sulfur does not register Tambora (max +1.5), so S is blind there. Identical ratio maxima in several years (Tl/Al +18.7, Bi/Al +4.5) look like below-detection replacement samples and are not evidence. Dasuopu has no 1816 samples, no 1815 Bi spike, and flagged ratio medians in 1809-11 that recur in every low-dust year, so it is not a validated detector. Inference: a weak negative that does not locate the source: the B62 band is withdrawn (B66), and since Oct-Feb tropical eruptions deposit preferentially north (B66) a Nov-Dec 1808 aerosol should have reached 28°N, so the null more likely reflects the magma's Bi yield, monsoon-season sampling or the detector's miss rate (the same core missed Krakatau 1883). Supersedes nothing; extends B67. U127 now downloaded and read. Age model (Xu 2009, text, 05:15): layer counting checked against Tambora, Agung and Pinatubo as horizons, so the 1815 Bi peak is partly built into the dating, but that pins 1809-10 within a year and no other year of 1800-1830 has a raw Bi maximum above z +2, so the null is not a dating miss; Xu's nine Bi events since 1800 (1815, 1845, 1911, 1919, 1921, 1953, 1963, 1967, 1991) include no 1809-10 peak and do include an 18°S eruption (Tonga 1846), and his recordable seasons (Dec-Apr, late May-Sep) cover a Nov-Dec eruption. Open: artefact check and 1815 season from the samples CSV, Dasuopu 1816 gap.


Addendum 05:25 (raw files uploaded by William, read in full; note section 9): below-detection replacement values identified (Bi, Tl, Al 0.02; S 0.15; U 3.0) and removed; Tambora is one sample at 1815.71 with Bi 196.7 ppt, EFc 3,882 (seven times the next-largest enrichment of 1700-1850, S below detection), five months after the eruption; the sixteen samples 1808.20-1811.40 have Bi 0.13-4.5 ppt and EFc 1.7-27 against a median of 20 and Xu's threshold of 371, which reproduces eight of Xu's nine published events from the file; a Tambora-scaled 1809 signal (about 130 ppt) or a tenth of it is absent; Dasuopu is continuous 1796-1815 and silent in 1809-11 but has no samples from 1815.80 to 1817.01 (missing 0.31 m), and its largest 1700-1850 enrichment, at 1823.62, may be Galunggung 1822 (suggestion). Derived CSV and figure in data/ (everest_samples_1795-1830_efc_2026-10-28.csv, himalaya_bismuth_1795-1830_2026-10-28.png); raw NOAA files kept out of the project folder (no original downloads in the repo).

### B69. Existing sediment, coral and speleothem archives within 300 km of the candidates (28 Oct 2026; notes/existing_cores_near_candidates_2026-10-28.md, data/existing_cores_near_candidates_2026-10-28.csv)

Option 8 of B62 executed. PANGAEA (through the DataONE index), DataCite and the NOAA paleo catalogue were swept in ±2.7° boxes around Dakataua, Gaua, Cabalían, Agung, Todoko-Ranu/Dukono, Teon/Nila and Mahawu/Lokon; 58 archives tabulated with great-circle distances [calc]. Result [text]: no archive within 300 km of any candidate has a tephra dated to c. 1809, and no catalogued marine core in the boxes has a published age model for the last centuries (PANGAEA holds MSCL and colour scans or long-period proxies only). The one basin where existing cores could resolve 1809 is Kau Bay, Halmahera (45-135 km from Todoko-Ranu/Dukono): dated core BJ8-03-102GGC (Langton et al. 2008; 377 m, 4.25 m, -1550 to 1950 CE, c. 8 yr/cm in the upper part but sampled every 8 cm), IMAGES core MD98-2180 (415 m, 19.6 m, MSCL only) and twelve Snellius-II piston cores of 1985 (progress report read in full: not laminated, no ash described). Agung has the only dense set of dated sub-decadal archives: Lakes Lading (Ca/Ti every 1.6-1.9 yr across 1800-1820, chronology ±30 yr), Logung (magnetic susceptibility yearly, geochemistry every 5-8 yr) and Ranu Lamongan (laminated), 237-244 km west, plus the Lombok Strait coral (10 km) and Kangean teak (165 km); the Rodysill 2013 abstract itself names eruptions of 1809, 1815 and 1835 as a cause of the drought it dates to 1790 ± 20 with a peak c. 1810 ± 30. Gaua: Lake Letas uncored; ODP 832/833 (59-79 km, 1990, top resolution unknown); the Espiritu Santo coral (annual from 1806, 97 km) and Taurius Cave stalagmite (3-month, 150 km) read: no 1809-11 cooling, the coral's only >1 sd years in 1806-22 are 1816, 1820, 1821 (positive, cool/dry, after Tambora) and the stalagmite shows a wet 1809-10 and drying after 1815 [calc]. Dakataua: nothing nearer than 230 km (MD97-2136/2137 undated; New Ireland Basin ash beds Late Quaternary; Kavieng and Rabaul corals start 1823 and 1867); no Lake Dakataua core. Cabalían: SO228 cores off Mindanao at 294-307 km only; new lead, a c. 800-yr reef-hole core near Bohol (Monica 2025 dissertation, 127 km, PDF blocked, U130). Southern Banda: nothing within 200 km of Teon/Nila; Timor Sea and north Banda cores 205-288 km, all long-period. North Sulawesi: Bunaken coral (1860-1990) only. Coverage caveat: the US IMLGS could not be queried from Claude's cloud container (U132), Neotoma's location filter failed. Added U99 and U138-U143 (D), U133-U137 (B), U130-U132 (A).

### B70. Baclayon (Bohol) burial book read as page images, February 1806 to December 1810: no spike after the eruption window, a mortality crisis in August-December 1810 (28 Oct 2026; notes/cabalian_parish_records_familysearch_2026-10-28.md sections 9-10, data/baclayon_burials_monthly_1806-1810_2026-10-28.csv, scripts/fs_film_fetch.py)
Extends B63 (option 10 of B62). William downloaded images 1475-1545 of FamilySearch digital film 7768815 (Baclayon, Purísima Concepción, Defunciones 1747-1868) with scripts/fs_film_fetch.py and his own Safari session; Claude read images 1475-1536 (book pp. 109v-170, 2 Feb 1806 to 3 Jan 1811) from his Mac folder as page images and counted every entry by month [page image; counts Claude's, one pass; table in the note and the CSV]. Results: (1) burials 1806 (Feb-Dec) 134, 1807 111, 1808 118, 1809 126; November 1808 (18, thirteen of them children) is the top month of 1808 but equals November 1806 (16) and is followed by 4-12 a month to April 1809: no excess mortality and no change in the gratis-burial rate in the months after a November-December 1808 eruption. (2) No marginal or added note on sky, ash, noise, earthquake, hunger or sickness anywhere in five years; the only non-burial texts are the episcopal visitations of 23 Apr 1807 and 8 Jul 1809 (formulaic) and the death of the coadjutor Remigio del Rosario on 22 Mar 1810 (no cause), all transcribed in the note. (3) A crisis of 142 burials in Aug-Dec 1810 (36, 39, 26, 22, 19) against 44-59 in those months of 1806-09: children first (Aug-Sep), then casados, viudas and reservados from October, gratis burials frequent, two siblings buried 5 and 6 Dec, no cause ever written; a smaller child-led wave in Feb-Mar 1806 (29, 21). Inference (Claude's): a weak negative for a very large eruption close to Bohol in Nov-Dec 1808 (Cabalían or any Visayan vent), silent on distant candidates; the 1810 crisis, twenty months after the window, fits an epidemic or a food crisis after the 1809-10 drought belt (B51, B60, B64) or both, and the book cannot say which. Tagbilaran (DGS 7769534), Loon (5382642) and Dauis (7768849) samples read the same way locate 1807-10 at images c. 2008-2075 (Tagbilaran) and 1360-1450 (Loon); Dauis jumps from 1792 to 1821 between images 1501 and 1551, so 1796-1820 is missing or elsewhere (U144); the ranges were ordered at 05:28Z and will be read the same way. Priority list and findings.csv unchanged until the other parishes are read. unavailable_sources.md: U121 amended, U144 added.

### B71. Tagbilaran and Loon burial books read 1807-1811, Baclayon followed to April 1811, Dauis film found to skip 1793-1812: no 1808-09 spike in any Bohol parish; an island-wide mortality crisis 1810-11 with a children's wave moving Baclayon-Tagbilaran-Loon in Jan-Apr 1811 (28 Oct 2026; notes/cabalian_parish_records_familysearch_2026-10-28.md section 11, notes/bohol_burial_entries_by_image_2026-10-28.md, data/bohol_burials_monthly_three_parishes_1806-1812_2026-10-28.csv)
Completes B70 (option 10 of B62). William downloaded Tagbilaran DGS 7769534 images 2008-2075, Loon DGS 5382642 images 1360-1450 and a Dauis DGS 7768849 sample at 05:32Z with scripts/fs_film_fetch.py; Claude read them from his Mac folder as page images and counted every entry by month [page image; counts Claude's, one pass]. Results: (1) Tagbilaran 1807 61, 1808 57, 1809 92, 1810 169, 1811 185; Loon 1807 50, 1808 51, 1809 47, 1810 96, 1811 110; no excess in Nov 1808-Apr 1809 in either (Tagbilaran Dec 1808 = 11 and Loon Nov 1808 = 8 are each the top month of an ordinary year; gratis burials unchanged). (2) No marginal or added note of sky, ash, noise, earthquake, hunger or sickness in either book 1806-1811: only the visitations (Tagbilaran 15 Apr 1807, Apr 1808, 17 Jul 1809; Loon 7 Apr 1807, 17 Aug 1809; Fr Pedro Asenjo de S. Antonio secretary, as at Baclayon), the death of Fr Joaquín de S. Josef at Tagbilaran on 25 Oct 1807, two killings at Loon (7 Sep 1807, 21 Jan 1810) and the usual sudden deaths. (3) Tagbilaran alone rises in 1809 (+50%, spread through the year, under two priests): undecided. (4) The 1810-11 crisis is island-wide and two years long: Loon from Mar 1810, Tagbilaran from Jul 1810, Baclayon from Aug 1810; a children's wave peaks at Baclayon in Jan 1811 (68 burials, almost all párvulos, from images 1536-1545 read today, which corrects the "January 1811 opens at the normal rate" of B70 and note sec. 10, read from the first two entries only), at Tagbilaran in Jan-Feb 1811 (23, c. 29) and at Loon in Mar-Apr 1811 (21, 18); Tagbilaran stays at twice its 1807-08 rate to Dec 1811 with "sin limosna" burials frequent from Aug 1811, and Loon has 19 burials in 1-18 Jan 1812. (5) Dauis: the film passes from May 1792 (image 1501) to Jul-Aug 1813 (image 1511, new book f. 7), so Dauis cannot serve the 1807-10 test (U144 amended). Inference (Claude's): a weak negative for a very large eruption near Bohol in Nov-Dec 1808, now from three parishes 35 km apart; silent on distant candidates. The 1810-11 crisis reads as hunger (adults and reservados in late 1810, rising gratis burials, two-year elevation, the 1809-10 drought belt at this latitude) with a contagious childhood disease moving east to west along the coast in Jan-Apr 1811 (suggestion: smallpox or measles; no source read names one); the books cannot separate the two. Priority list and findings.csv unchanged. unavailable_sources.md: U121 to downloaded and read, U144 amended, U145, U146 and U89 added. Mac folder access no longer needed for this strand.

### B72. The National Archives (Kew) record copying route tested on the HMS Cornwallis captain's log ADM 51/2046: page check complete, 60 A3 digital images for 1 Nov 1808-31 Mar 1809 quoted at £91.20 (28 Oct 2026; notes/tna_record_copying_test_cornwallis_2026-10-28.md)
William lodged a TNA page check (order RC8585845) on ADM 51/2046 (U70, the top Kew item: HMS Cornwallis, 28 Jul 1808-8 Feb 1811; B48, section E), asking for pages on weather, winds, visibility, sea state and unusual atmospheric phenomena between 1 Nov 1808 and 31 Mar 1809. TNA's reply on the order page (exported from Safari as a PDF on 28 Oct 2026 and read by Claude as a page image; Claude could not view Safari) quotes the whole run of dated entries for those five months rather than a selection: 60 A3 digital images, blanks removed, "research-quality" reduced-size JPEG at 300 ppi, £91.20, paper copies not offered. The price is 60 × £1.52, TNA's published fee for digital copies up to A3 (page check £9.92 per record; fees page fetched 28 Oct 2026 [text]). The order had not been added to the basket when captured; nothing was ordered and no letter was written. TNA's record copying page says the whole process takes "approximately 29 working days from start to finish" and that demand is "very high" [text]; the page check itself came back by 28 Oct, within two working days of U70 being listed if it was lodged on or after 27 Oct (lodging date to be confirmed by William). Assessment [Claude's judgement]: the route is proven through the quote stage and cheap (about £100 per five-month log window), and it reaches U71-U76 the same way; the unknowns are the copy-stage turnaround and the legibility of 300 ppi JPEGs. Psyche (U73, at Surigao in 1809, across the strait from Cabalían) is the natural second test. Files: archives/TNA_ADM_51-2046_Cornwallis_log/ in the Mac project folder. U70 status changed to "requires online request".

**Correction (28 Oct 2026, 22:50 UTC; note section 6).** William reports the page check took about a week (lodged c. 21 Oct), not the two working days guessed above. More important: the itinerary "inside the Moluccas and Celebes Jan-Mar 1809" that put ADM 51/2046 at the top of the Kew list (catalogue sweep 16 Oct, "[inferred, Wikipedia]"; U70; B48) is a year out. Marshall's *Royal Naval Biography* dates Montagu's Boolo-Combo letter 17 Jan 1810, the Manippa cutting-out 1 Feb 1810 and the *Margaretta* capture 2 Mar 1810, all in the Amboyna campaign whose force left Madras on 9 Oct 1809; Fleetwood Pellew had left the ship for Phaeton in July 1808, so the log from 28 Jul 1808 is Montagu's. For the quoted window the TNA catalogue of ADM 1 letters places Cornwallis at Madras in Oct 1808 (ADM 1/1651/63) and in the Persian Gulf in Jun 1809 (ADM 1/1547/245); by inference she spent Nov 1808-Mar 1809 on the Bombay-Gulf side of the station, far from every candidate. U70 yield lowered to L-M; the £91.20 order is not recommended; catalogue_sweep_1808-09.md lines 12 and 65 marked as corrected. Prior analysis of such logs: Chenoweth 2001 used ships' marine air temperatures 1807-27 (and the Penang register, U77) to find the 1809 cooling and date the eruption to Mar-Jun 1808; Brohan et al. 2012 digitised EIC logs for temperature and wind; CLIWOC holds no 1808-09 record in the Celebes-Moluccas box [calc]; no study known here has read Royal Navy logs of the eastern islands for ash, darkness or haze. Same catalogue method for the other Kew ships: Phaeton at Penang 22 Jan 1809 after her passage from Nagasaki (U74), Dover sent after Russell to Macao in Sep 1808 (U72), nothing found for Sir Francis Drake (U71) or Psyche (U73). Suggestion: track-check those three the same way, then page-check the ones east of Java in the window.

### B73. Tracks of the Kew logs for the eruption window checked from catalogue, Horsburgh and biographies: Psyche in the Philippines Apr-Oct 1809 (Surigao, 70 km from Cabalían) is the best copying target; Phaeton second; Sir Francis Drake and Cornwallis west of India (28 Oct 2026; notes/tna_record_copying_test_cornwallis_2026-10-28.md section 7)
At William's word after the B72 correction, the Nov 1808-Mar 1809 tracks of Phaeton (U74), Sir Francis Drake (U71) and Psyche (U73) were checked without ordering anything: TNA Discovery item descriptions of ADM 1 letters and the ADM 51/52 log volumes [catalogue text], Horsburgh's *India Directory* OCR (1836, 1841, 1852 editions on the Internet Archive) [text], Marshall's and O'Byrne's biographies on Wikisource and Wikipedia campaign articles [text], James vol. 5 OCR (no 1809 mention). Results: (1) Phaeton: Nagasaki 4-6 Oct 1808, at Penang by 21 Jan 1809 (master's log ADM 52/3853 ends; ADM 1/2334/117), Nov-Dec 1808 unfixed, probably Macao and the South China Sea [inference]; a veil check at 10-22°N only; yield M. (2) Sir Francis Drake: Maldives 27 Jul 1808, shoal off Mount Ophir (west Sumatra) 1 Sep 1809, Cargados Carajos Jan 1810, off Java only from Aug 1810; the U71 "Java/Madura blockade" is 1810-11; window position unverified, probably Bay of Bengal or Sumatra; yield L. (3) Psyche: Malabar coast Dec 1808-Feb 1809 (Travancore campaign with Piemontaise; RNB; Horsburgh vol. 1 Cape Comorin soundings), then with Cole's Doris to Manila and Macao in search of two French frigates, Apr-Oct 1809 (RNB; ADM 1/1940/54 Cole at Penang 3 Apr 1809; ADM 1/1453/50 Doris and Psyche in the China seas Oct 1809); Horsburgh vol. 2 p. 520 has her observations at Surigao, Mindanao, in 1809 and her master Maitland's plan of the bay. Surigao is about 70 km from Cabalían, so the master's log ADM 52/4576 (Psyche 13 Apr-13 Oct 1809) is the one Kew volume that could test that candidate directly, 2-8 months after the ice-core onset window; yield raised to H. Suggestion: page-check ADM 52/4576 first (about six months, order of £100), then ADM 51/2666 for the same months; Phaeton ADM 51/1952 for Oct 1808-Jan 1809 second; Cornwallis and Sir Francis Drake not for the window. U71, U73 and U74 updated. Nothing ordered; no letter written.
